**Mulder Shipyard: A Comprehensive Profile for DataYacht.com**
*(Word count: ~2,450)*

### Introduction: Timeless Dutch Craftsmanship in Modern Yachting

Mulder Shipyard stands as one of the Netherlands’ most respected family-owned yacht builders, blending nearly nine decades of heritage with contemporary innovation. Founded in 1938, the company has earned a reputation for constructing elegant, seaworthy aluminum motor yachts that prioritize family comfort, timeless aesthetics, and reliable performance. Unlike some larger conglomerates focused purely on scale, Mulder maintains an intimate, boutique approach — delivering semi-custom and fully custom vessels where every detail reflects the owner’s vision while benefiting from proven naval architecture and engineering.

Today, under the leadership of third-generation Managing Director **Nick Mulder**, the shipyard continues to thrive by emphasizing “Crafting Memories” — the core philosophy that yachts should enhance quality time spent with loved ones on the water. This family-centric ethos permeates everything from design to after-sales service, setting Mulder apart in a competitive landscape dominated by volume builders and ultra-exclusive custom houses.

For data platforms like DataYacht.com, Mulder represents a compelling mid-to-upper luxury segment player: yachts typically ranging from 13 to 50 meters, with strong emphasis on the 18–45 meter bracket. Their success in the ThirtySix series demonstrates proven market demand for efficient, award-winning displacement-style superyachts that balance luxury with practicality.

### Rich History: From Humble Beginnings to Superyacht Excellence

The story of Mulder Shipyard begins in 1938 when **Dirk Mulder** established a small metal workshop in Voorschoten. Driven by a personal passion for family boating, Dirk built his first vessel to create joyful experiences on the water with his wife and children. This foundational motivation — prioritizing family time — remains the guiding principle today.

In the post-war years, the yard specialized in wooden sailboats and small motor craft before transitioning to steel and eventually aluminum construction. By the 1980s, under Dirk’s son **Dick Mulder** (who took leadership at age 22 in 1984), the company expanded into larger full-custom motor yachts up to 28 meters. The introduction of iconic lines like the **Super Favorite Cruisers** and later **Favorite Superieur** solidified Mulder’s reputation for classic, robust Dutch-built vessels.

The third generation, **Nick Mulder**, assumed full leadership around 2022 following his father’s passing. Nick has modernized operations while preserving the family ethos. Key milestones include:

- 2013: Opening of the second major yard in Zoeterwoude-Rijndijk.
- 2022: Acquisition of a facility in Enkhuizen, bringing the total to four specialized locations.
- 2024–2026: Aggressive expansion into the US market via Mulder USA and strategic acquisitions (e.g., nautical interiors firm Neptune in April 2026).

This continuity of family ownership provides stability and client trust that many corporate yards cannot match. Mulder has delivered hundreds of yachts over 85+ years, evolving from modest cruisers to award-winning 36-meter-plus superyachts.

### State-of-the-Art Facilities Across Four Dutch Locations

Mulder operates four specialized sites, creating a highly efficient full-service ecosystem for new construction, refit, maintenance, and storage.

- **Zoeterwoude-Rijndijk (Headquarters, opened 2013)**: The primary facility for yachts 18–45 meters. Focuses on new builds, major refits, and maintenance. Features advanced aluminum construction capabilities, dry docks, and outfitting halls. This is where most ThirtySix series yachts are completed.

- **Voorschoten (Original Yard)**: Dedicated to smaller yachts (up to ~18 meters) and ongoing maintenance/storage. Recently renovated for efficiency.

- **Enkhuizen (Acquired 2022)**: Specialized aluminum hull construction and engineering. This northern facility enhances capacity for larger projects and provides geographic diversification.

- **Warmond (Mulder Marina)**: Supports storage, launching, and owner experiences with direct water access.

Collectively, these yards employ approximately 70–80 skilled craftsmen and engineers. The multi-site model allows parallel workflows — hull fabrication in one location, interior outfitting in another — enabling faster build times while maintaining quality control. Recent investments include expanded terrain (6,000 m² purchased for further growth) and modern equipment for sustainable practices.

### Signature Product Lines: Versatility and Timeless Appeal

Mulder’s portfolio spans day boats to superyachts, with several core series:

**Favorite Series (13–22+ meters)**:
Classic dayboat/cruiser designs with spacious cabins, bright saloons, and large cockpits. Popular models include the **Favorite 1500**, **Favorite 1700**, and newer **Favorite 2000** and **2200**. These are ideal for coastal cruising and family outings. In early 2026, a new Favorite 1500 was sold and entered build for a repeat client. Over 20 Favorite 1500 units have been delivered.

**Convertible Line (54–72 feet)**:
Combines wheelhouse comfort with open-air freedom. Practical for owners who enjoy both protected and open-bridge cruising.

**Wheelhouse Line (53–76 feet)**:
Mulder’s most popular range historically. Offers excellent all-weather capability with enclosed wheelhouses and spacious interiors.

**Flybridge Line (73–98 feet)**:
Features slim bows and sweeping hulls for performance and seakeeping in varied conditions.

**ThirtySix Series (36 meters / 118 feet)**:
The flagship semi-custom platform and standout success story. Launched in the mid-2010s, this series has delivered or has in-build nearly 10 hulls by mid-2026. Key highlights:

- **Naval Architecture**: Van Oossanen’s patented **Fast Displacement Hull Form (FDHF)** — delivers efficient cruising at 11–15 knots with low fuel consumption (under 80 liters/hour at 11 knots) and a top speed of ~17 knots.
- **Dimensions**: 36m LOA, 8m beam, shallow draft suitable for varied destinations.
- **Layout**: Five luxurious cabins (10 guests), full-beam master with private bow access, expansive windows, folding balcony, beach club with wet bar, 7-person spa pool on flybridge, transverse tender garage (6.25m tender + toys).
- **Gross Tonnage**: Under 300GT, offering regulatory advantages.
- **Notable Hulls** (as of 2026): CASTLEFINN (2025, Hull 8/9), Q43 (2024), SEAFLOWER (2023), SOLEMATES (2022), MY WAY (2021), JOSEPHINE (2020), CALYPSO I (2019), DELTA ONE (2017). Hull 9 began sea trials in May 2026 ahead of spring delivery.

The ThirtySix has won multiple **World Superyacht Awards**, including Best Displacement Motor Yacht categories, validating its blend of performance, comfort, and style.

### Awards, Notable Launches & Market Recognition

Mulder yachts consistently earn acclaim:

- **SEAFLOWER** (ThirtySix, 2023): Best Displacement Motor Yacht <500GT at 2024 World Superyacht Awards.
- Multiple wins for vessels like SOLIS, DELTA ONE, and CALYPSO I.
- Recognized for interior innovation and overall Dutch craftsmanship.

Recent activity includes strong US market push, with yachts like CASTLEFINN debuting at Palm Beach and Fort Lauderdale shows. Mulder USA (partnership with Luxury Yacht Group, launched 2024) targets American clients seeking reliable, family-oriented superyachts.

### Current Market Position & Brokerage Insights

Mulder yachts typically occupy the **€2–20 million+** range depending on size and specification. As of 2026, brokerage listings include award-winners like **SEAFLOWER** (€18.9M) and **SOLIS** (€9.995M). The secondary market shows healthy demand, particularly for well-maintained Favorites and ThirtySix hulls.

**Strengths**:
- Dutch build quality and engineering reliability.
- Efficient hull designs reducing operating costs.
- Strong family/refit focus creates loyal client base.
- Full-service ecosystem (new build + brokerage + refit + charter).

**Opportunities**:
- Continued US expansion.
- Growth into 40–50m segment.
- Sustainability upgrades (hybrid options emerging in Dutch yards).

**Challenges**:
- Competition from larger players like Heesen, Feadship, and Van der Valk in similar size ranges.
- Family succession planning (though current leadership appears stable).

### Why Mulder Matters for DataYacht.com

Mulder Shipyard exemplifies the enduring appeal of family-owned Dutch yachting: quality over quantity, heritage with innovation, and a genuine focus on owner lifestyle. Their track record — from 13m Favorites to multiple 36m superyachts — provides rich data points on build times (typically 24–36 months for semi-custom), resale values, refit potential, and charter performance.

For analysts, brokers, and enthusiasts using DataYacht.com, Mulder offers a benchmark in the 20–40 meter aluminum semi-displacement category. The yard’s transparency, consistent output, and recent international momentum suggest continued growth through 2030.

**References** (selected key sources):
- Official Mulder Shipyard website (muldershipyard.nl)
- Boat International profiles and news
- YachtBuyer, SuperYacht Times, MegaYacht News
- World Superyacht Awards records

### Executive Summary and Market Positioning

Mulder Shipyard remains one of the Netherlands’ premier family-owned yacht builders, renowned for crafting elegant, seaworthy aluminum motor yachts that emphasize timeless design, family comfort, and operational efficiency. Established in 1938, the yard has evolved from modest beginnings into a respected name in the 13–50 meter segment, with particular strength in semi-custom superyachts. Under third-generation leadership, Mulder balances heritage with strategic growth, including expansion into the US market and vertical integration through acquisitions.

For DataYacht.com users tracking brokerage trends, refit opportunities, new construction data, and competitive analysis, Mulder offers valuable benchmarks: reliable Dutch build quality, strong resale values, efficient hull forms, and a loyal owner base focused on family cruising rather than pure opulence. Their flagship **ThirtySix** series has proven exceptionally successful, with nine hulls launched or in advanced construction by mid-2026.

### Deep History: Three Generations of Family Commitment

Mulder Shipyard’s story is deeply rooted in family values. In 1938, **Dirk Mulder** founded the company in a small metal workshop in Voorschoten with a simple goal: to build boats that would allow quality time on the water with his wife and children. This ethos — “Crafting Memories” — continues to define the yard today.

The post-war period saw Mulder transition from wooden sailboats and small motor craft to steel and then aluminum construction. In 1984, at just 22 years old, **Dick Mulder** (second generation) took the helm and steered the company toward larger full-custom motor yachts up to 28 meters. Iconic early lines such as the **Super Favorite Cruisers** and **Favorite Superieur** established Mulder’s reputation for classic, robust Dutch vessels suited for real-world family use.

**Nick Mulder** assumed leadership around 2022 following his father’s passing. With over 15 years of hands-on experience, Nick has focused on modernization, capacity expansion, and international outreach while preserving the family-centric philosophy. His recent acquisition of nautical interiors specialist **Neptune** (April 2026) vertically integrates supply chains and enhances customization capabilities.

This unbroken family ownership provides clients with continuity and trust uncommon in an industry increasingly dominated by private equity and large groups.

### Facilities: A Strategic Four-Site Ecosystem

Mulder operates four specialized locations in the Netherlands, creating an efficient full-service operation for new builds, refits, maintenance, and storage.

- **Zoeterwoude-Rijndijk (opened 2013)**: The flagship headquarters spanning over 7,000 m². Dedicated to yachts 18–45+ meters, new construction, major refits, and outfitting. This is the primary site for ThirtySix series builds and recent dry-dock maneuvers involving multiple large yachts simultaneously.

- **Voorschoten**: The original yard, focused on smaller vessels (up to ~18–20 meters), routine maintenance, and storage. Recently renovated for improved efficiency.

- **Enkhuizen (acquired 2022)**: Specialized aluminum hull fabrication and engineering. This northern facility boosts capacity for larger projects and provides redundancy.

- **Warmond (Mulder Marina)**: Supports owner experiences, launching, winter storage, and summer cruises with direct water access.

The multi-site model enables parallel workflows — hulls built in Enkhuizen, interiors and final assembly in Zoeterwoude — shortening build times while maintaining strict quality control. In recent years, Mulder purchased an additional 6,000 m² terrain for further expansion.

The yard employs approximately 70–80 skilled professionals. Annual turnover has been reported around €40 million (2023 figures), with a healthy order book often including 8–10 projects in various stages.

### Core Product Lines: From Day Boats to Award-Winning Superyachts

Mulder’s range covers day cruisers to full superyachts, with strong emphasis on practical luxury.

**Favorite Series (13–22+ meters)**:
Timeless dayboat and cruiser designs featuring classic lines, bright interiors, spacious cockpits, and comfortable cabins. Popular models include the **Favorite 1500** (15.6m), with over 20 units delivered. In late 2025, a new Favorite 1500 was sold to a repeat client for Mediterranean use, with 2026 delivery. Newer variants like the Favorite 2000 and 2200 continue the line’s success.

**Convertible, Wheelhouse, and Flybridge Lines**:
These mid-size ranges (roughly 16–30 meters) offer flexibility — open freedom combined with protected wheelhouses or expansive flybridges — making them ideal for varied cruising conditions and family lifestyles.

**ThirtySix Series (36 meters / 118 feet)**:
The crown jewel and most commercially successful platform. Using Van Oossanen’s patented **Fast Displacement Hull Form (FDHF)**, these yachts achieve efficient cruising (11–15 knots) with excellent fuel economy and a top speed around 17 knots. Gross tonnage stays under 300GT for regulatory advantages.

Key specifications typically include:
- LOA: 36m, Beam: 8m
- 5 cabins (owner’s full-beam master often with private bow terrace), accommodation for 10 guests + crew
- Features: Beach club, spa pool, folding balcony, tender garage for 6.25m tender + toys
- High customisation while retaining proven naval architecture

**Documented Hulls (as of May 2026)**:
- Hull 1: Delta One (2017)
- Hulls 2–4: Early deliveries
- Hull 5: Solemates (2022)
- Hull 6/7: Q43 (2024), others
- Hull 8: Castlefinn (delivered ~April/May 2025 to Canadian owners)
- Hull 9: Launched March 2026, sea trials began May 11, 2026; spring delivery expected.

Multiple ThirtySix yachts have undergone refits or resales, demonstrating strong aftermarket activity.

### Awards and Industry Recognition

Mulder has collected multiple **World Superyacht Awards** (Boat International), including:
- **Seaflower** (2024): Best Displacement Motor Yacht below 500GT
- **Delta One** (2018)
- **Solis** (2016)
- **Calypso I** (2020): Best Interior Design & Innovation

These wins highlight consistent excellence in construction quality, family-friendly layouts, and tasteful custom interiors.

### Market Presence, Brokerage, and Sales Insights

Mulder yachts typically range from €1–3 million for Favorite models to €12–20+ million for ThirtySix series, depending on specification and condition.

Notable 2025–2026 brokerage examples:
- **Seaflower** (2023): €18.9 million
- **Solis** (2015): €9.995 million (with price reductions noted)
- **Mahalo** (30m, 2014): Offered around €4.9 million

The secondary market shows healthy demand, particularly for well-maintained Dutch-built aluminum yachts with proven reliability. Mulder’s in-house brokerage supports transparency and owner transitions.

**US Market Push**: In October 2024, Mulder launched **Mulder USA** in partnership with Luxury Yacht Group. This has already resulted in strong American interest, with yachts like Castlefinn debuting at US shows (Palm Beach, Fort Lauderdale). American clients particularly appreciate the family focus and efficient operation costs.

### Strengths, Opportunities, and Competitive Context

**Strengths**:
- Family ownership and continuity
- Efficient, proven hull designs lowering long-term costs
- Full-service capabilities (new build + refit + brokerage + marina)
- Strong awards track record and owner loyalty

**Opportunities**:
- Further US and international growth
- Hybrid/sustainable technology integration (common trend in Dutch yards)
- Expansion into slightly larger 40–50m semi-custom projects
- Leveraging Neptune acquisition for faster, higher-quality interiors

**Challenges**:
- Competition in the 30–40m segment from yards like Heesen, Van der Valk, and Moonen
- Maintaining growth while preserving boutique quality
- Broader industry issues like skilled labor availability

### Why Mulder Matters for DataYacht.com Analysis

Mulder Shipyard exemplifies the sweet spot in luxury yachting: vessels that are luxurious yet practical, custom yet proven. Their data points — build times of 24–36 months for semi-custom projects, strong charter performance of ThirtySix hulls, consistent resale values, and refit activity — provide rich insights for market reports, valuation models, and client recommendations.

The yard’s 2025–2026 momentum (Hull 9 sea trials, new Favorite sales, US show presence, Neptune acquisition) signals continued upward trajectory through the decade. Mulder is not chasing volume but delivering memorable, seaworthy yachts that owners actually use extensively with family and friends.

### Executive Summary and Market Positioning

Heesen Yachts stands as one of the Netherlands’ most dynamic and respected superyacht builders, renowned for high-performance aluminum and steel motor yachts that combine exceptional speed, refined luxury, and fuel-efficient cruising. Founded in 1978, the Oss-based shipyard has launched over 200 yachts, many of which have set benchmarks in naval architecture, design innovation, and award-winning craftsmanship. Heesen excels in the 40–70+ meter segment, offering a compelling mix of **full-custom**, **smart-custom**, and **series-built** platforms that appeal to experienced owners seeking performance without compromising comfort or style.

For DataYacht.com users analyzing brokerage trends, new construction pipelines, refit potential, and competitive dynamics, Heesen provides rich data points: rapid build capabilities (some yachts delivered in under 24 months), strong secondary market performance, and a focus on technically advanced hull forms. Under new ownership since 2025, the yard enters 2026 with robust momentum — multiple deliveries scheduled, a healthy order book, and continued innovation in efficient displacement and semi-displacement designs.

Heesen positions itself in the upper-luxury performance segment, competing with yards like Lürssen, Benetti, and Amels, but distinguishing itself through Dutch engineering precision, in-house capabilities, and a track record of fast, reliable vessels.

### Rich History: From Entrepreneurial Vision to Global Leadership

Heesen Yachts was established in 1978 by Dutch entrepreneur **Frans Heesen**, who acquired the former Striker Boats shipyard along a canal in Oss after building success in the plastics industry. What began as a potential diversification quickly evolved into a dedicated yacht-building venture focused on high-performance aluminum motor yachts. Heesen was among the first Dutch yards to specialize in aluminum hulls for larger vessels, pioneering lightweight, fast designs that became a hallmark of the brand.

The 1980s and 1990s saw rapid growth with iconic sportfishing-inspired models and performance cruisers. By the early 2000s, Heesen expanded into larger superyachts, delivering groundbreaking vessels like the award-winning *Satori* (2011) and *Galactica Star* (2013), the latter reaching nearly 29 knots. Frans Heesen retired in 2012 after 35 years at the helm, having built a legacy of innovation and quality.

In 2022, following external ownership challenges related to sanctions, the company transitioned to an independent Dutch foundation to protect operations and jobs. A pivotal change came in April 2025 when Dutch billionaire and yachtsman **Laurens Last** acquired Heesen through his family office, TSAL Holding. This return to fully Dutch private ownership has injected fresh energy while preserving the workforce and core values. Laurens Last’s vision emphasizes strengthening financial performance and continuing Heesen’s upward trajectory after record revenues in 2024.

Today, the yard benefits from nearly five decades of accumulated knowledge, with a strong emphasis on in-house engineering, naval architecture partnerships (e.g., Van Oossanen for Fast Displacement Hull Forms), and collaborations with top designers like Omega Architects, Cristiano Gatto, and Luca Dini.

### State-of-the-Art Facilities: Integrated Excellence in Oss and Beyond

Heesen’s primary shipyard is located in **Oss, southern Netherlands**, spanning a substantial footprint with advanced infrastructure optimized for large-scale superyacht construction. The facility includes **nine production halls** and **five dry docks**, one of which is an 85-meter dry dock added in 2016, enabling construction of yachts up to 83 meters. This setup supports efficient parallel workflows for multiple projects.

Key capabilities at the Oss yard:
- Aluminum and steel hull fabrication.
- Full outfitting, testing, and sea trials in the North Sea.
- Advanced environmental and efficiency technologies.

Complementing the main yard is the **Winterswijk interiors workshop**, expanded to over 8,100 m². This dedicated facility handles increasingly complex custom interiors, allowing Heesen to maintain vertical integration and tight quality control even as yachts grow in size and sophistication.

The combined facilities support a workforce of approximately **1,000 skilled professionals** across engineering, craftsmanship, and support roles. Recent investments have increased overall capacity by around 30%, enabling the yard to handle up to eight superyachts simultaneously while delivering high standards. This infrastructure underpins Heesen’s reputation for on-time or early deliveries and meticulous finishing.

### Signature Product Lines and Notable Launches

Heesen offers versatility across performance-oriented series and full-custom projects:

**50–55m Steel Class (Displacement/Semi-Displacement)**:
Highly successful series known for efficient cruising, spacious layouts, and strong seakeeping. Recent examples include **Project Frida** (now *Pa’lante*), a 55m steel yacht launched in August 2025 with delivery in February 2026. Features include an Ultra Efficient Steel Hull, interiors by Luca Dini, and accommodation for 12 guests. Multiple 55m steel hulls are in build, offering relatively fast delivery options.

Larger, more voluminous options with innovative features. *Santosha* (ex-Project Akira) won a prestigious **World Superyacht Award** in 2025 for its groundbreaking design as the first in a new 57m all-aluminum class.

**Full-Custom Projects**:
Bespoke creations like the 60m *Ultra G* (top speed 37 knots, inspired by 1990s sportfishers) and concepts such as Project Avanti, Project Ceres, XV67 hybrid explorer, and Project Monte-Carlo. These push boundaries in speed, hybrid propulsion, and owner-specific layouts.

**Notable Recent Activity (2025–2026)**:

The first yacht, launched in 1979, measured 28 meters, reached 28 knots, and set an early standard for speed and luxury.

Throughout the 1980s and 1990s, Heesen gained recognition for sportfishing-inspired designs and performance cruisers. Founded in 1873, the shipyard has delivered more than 430 superyachts, including multiple gigayachts, establishing it as a leader in the global mega-yacht segment. As the flagship brand of the **Azimut|Benetti Group**, Benetti combines heritage with modern innovation, offering composite, aluminum, and steel constructions across distinct lines such as Class, Oasis, B.Now, B.Yond, B.Loft, Motopanfilo, Custom, and the new B.Neos.

In 2026, Benetti continues its dominance as part of the world’s largest yacht-building group by volume and order book. Under the leadership of the Vitelli family (with Giulia Vitelli as Chairwoman following Paolo Vitelli’s passing in late 2024), the yard benefits from strong financials — the Group reported €1.5 billion in revenue for 2024/25 (+15%) with orders extending to 2029. Benetti’s focus on hybrid propulsion, sustainability, AI-driven production, and comprehensive after-sales services (including the new Azimut|Benetti Service Division launched in January 2026) positions it perfectly for owners seeking enduring luxury and operational excellence.

For DataYacht.com users analyzing brokerage trends, new construction pipelines, refit potential, resale values, and competitive landscapes, Benetti provides extensive data: high volume of deliveries, strong secondary market performance, diverse size offerings, and a loyal international clientele. Compared to Dutch builders like Mulder (family-oriented, 13–50m focus) or Heesen (performance-driven, 40–80m), Benetti excels in volume production of elegant, voluminous Italian superyachts with broader appeal in the 30–100m+ segment.

### In-Depth History: 153 Years of Italian Maritime Excellence

Benetti’s story is one of the longest and richest in yachting history. The shipyard was founded in **1873** by **Lorenzo Benetti** (1844–1914) in the Darsena area of Viareggio (later expanding to Livorno and Fano). Lorenzo purchased the existing Cantiere Darsena Lucca shipyard and began building wooden commercial vessels, fishing boats, and Mediterranean trading ships. This foundational focus on robust, practical vessels established the yard’s reputation for quality construction.

After Lorenzo’s death in 1914, his sons **Gino** and **Emilio Benetti** took over, renaming the company **Fratelli Benetti** (Brothers Benetti). They expanded into ocean-going ships and introduced some private yachts while continuing primarily wooden construction. The family business grew with subsequent generations — Giuseppe, Virgilio, Maurizio, and others — joining the operation. By the mid-20th century, the yard had shifted toward larger projects, building its first notable motor yachts in the 1960s.

A pivotal era began in the 1960s–1970s under family leadership, with Benetti gaining international fame for steel-hulled luxury yachts. The 282-foot (86m) **Nabila** (delivered in the early 1980s) became one of the largest private yachts in the world at the time, owned by Adnan Khashoggi and later by Donald Trump (as Trump Princess). This project cemented Benetti’s status among the global elite.

In **1985**, **Paolo Vitelli** — founder of Azimut Yachts (established 1969) — acquired the struggling Fratelli Benetti shipyard. This marked a new chapter: financial restructuring, modernization, and integration into what became the **Azimut|Benetti Group**. Paolo Vitelli’s vision transformed Benetti into a leader in semi-custom and custom superyachts while preserving its heritage. The Group grew rapidly, becoming the world’s top mega-yacht builder by order book for over 20 consecutive years.

Paolo Vitelli passed away in December 2024 at age 77 following an accident. His daughter, **Giulia Vitelli**, succeeded him as Chairwoman, continuing the family-led entrepreneurial spirit. Key recent milestones include AI integration in production (2025), opening of new Americas headquarters in Fort Lauderdale (2025), and the launch of the dedicated Service Division (January 2026).

### State-of-the-Art Facilities: Multiple Italian Centers of Excellence

Benetti operates across three primary Italian locations, supported by the Azimut|Benetti Group’s extensive infrastructure:

- **Viareggio**: Historic headquarters and key construction site, focused on composite and smaller-to-mid-size yachts.
- **Livorno**: Major facility for larger steel and aluminum superyachts, including gigayacht projects. Recently modernized with expanded capabilities for construction, refit, and outfitting. Hosts events like the Benetti Yachtmaster.
- **Fano**: Specialized production and engineering support.

The yards feature advanced facilities for composite, aluminum, and steel construction, with significant investments in sustainable technologies, hybrid systems, and AI-optimized production processes. The Group’s **Lusben** division handles major refits and repairs, providing full lifecycle support. Total workforce across the Group exceeds 1,800–2,000 professionals, enabling high-volume, high-quality output.

This multi-site model supports parallel builds, efficient workflows, and comprehensive after-sales services, including preventive maintenance and global assistance.

### Signature Product Lines and Recent Activity (2025–2026)

Benetti consistently earns top honors at World Superyacht Awards, Robb Report Best of the Best, and regional events. Notable winners include Luminosity (largest hybrid yacht, multiple awards), IJE (108m), Lana (Yacht of the Year), Spectre (69m), and many others. Benetti yachts typically range from €10–30 million for 30–50m semi-custom models to €50–200+ million for larger custom and gigayachts. The secondary market is robust, with strong resale values due to timeless design and quality construction. Examples include 40m Wabash (~$16.45M) and 37m Allora (~$7.5M).

As a collaborative partnership between Royal Van Lent Shipyard (est. 1849), Koninklijke De Vries Scheepsbouw (est. 1906), and De Voogt Naval Architects (est. 1913), Feadship combines over 400 years of Dutch shipbuilding heritage with modern innovation.

In 2026, Feadship continues to lead in the ultra-luxury custom segment with groundbreaking projects like the record-breaking *Breakthrough* (118.8m), the largest yacht ever built in the Netherlands and winner of **Motor Yacht of the Year** at the 2026 World Superyacht Awards. The yard maintains a strong order book (around 18 projects in build/on order as of early 2026) and pushes boundaries in sustainability, including hydrogen fuel-cell technology.

For DataYacht.com users analyzing brokerage trends, new construction pipelines, refit potential, resale values, and competitive intelligence, Feadship offers premium benchmarks: exceptional build quality, strong value retention, long-term owner loyalty, and innovation in propulsion and design. Unlike semi-custom-focused builders like Benetti or performance-oriented Heesen, Feadship excels in one-of-a-kind creations for the most discerning clients, often commanding the highest price points in the Dutch market.

### In-Depth History: Over 175 Years of Dutch Maritime Mastery

Feadship’s roots trace back to the 19th century. **Royal Van Lent Shipyard** was founded in 1849 on Kaag Island, initially building and repairing boats. **Koninklijke De Vries Scheepsbouw** began in 1906 in Aalsmeer under Christoffel Johannes Wilhelmus (Han) de Vries. These family-run yards specialized in high-quality construction.

In **1949**, De Vries and Van Lent joined four other Dutch yards and naval architect **Henri W. de Voogt** to form Feadship (First Export Association of Dutch Shipbuilders) at Café De Roode Leeuw in Amsterdam. The goal was to market superior Dutch yachts to American clients, leveraging post-war export incentives. De Voogt’s unified design approach quickly defined the brand’s elegant, seaworthy style.

Key milestones:
- **1950s–1970s**: Early exports to the US, building reputation with classic motor yachts for clients like Henry Ford and Malcolm Forbes.
- **1980s–2000s**: Expansion into larger superyachts, with iconic deliveries like *Ecstasea* and *Venus*.
- **2001 & 2006**: Royal designations granted — Van Lent became Royal Van Lent, De Vries became Koninklijke De Vries.
- **2010s–2020s**: Major facility upgrades, including the eco-friendly Amsterdam yard opened by Queen Máxima in 2019. Leadership includes figures like Henk de Vries and Jan Bart Verkuyl.

Feadship has delivered over 180 yachts over 30 meters, many record-breaking at their time. The partnership structure preserves family involvement while enabling large-scale custom projects. Recent focus includes sustainability and next-generation talent sponsorship (e.g., Young Designer of the Year Award 2026).

### State-of-the-Art Facilities: A Network of Dutch Excellence

Feadship operates multiple specialized facilities across the Netherlands, enabling efficient, high-quality custom construction:

- **De Vries Facilities**: Headquarters in Aalsmeer (smaller to mid-size yachts) and Makkum (dedicated to the largest projects, including 100m+ vessels with extensive outfitting halls).
- **Royal Van Lent Facilities**: Historic yard on Kaag Island and modern Amsterdam facility (opened 2019, eco-conscious design with advanced capabilities).
- **De Voogt Naval Architects**: Design and engineering center in Haarlem/Hoofddorp, responsible for naval architecture and innovation.

The multi-site model supports parallel workflows for hull construction, outfitting, and testing. Total workforce exceeds 1,300 skilled professionals. Facilities emphasize sustainability, with investments in green technologies and efficient processes. This infrastructure underpins Feadship’s reputation for meticulous craftsmanship and on-time deliveries of highly complex custom yachts.

### Signature Product Lines, Notable Launches, and 2025–2026 Activity

As a pure custom builder, Feadship has no fixed “series,” but recurring themes include elegant lines, advanced hull forms, expansive beach clubs, and owner-specific innovations. Every project starts from a blank sheet.

- **Breakthrough (118.8m, Project 821)**: Delivered 2025/2026 — the world’s first large liquid hydrogen fuel-cell superyacht. Winner of **Motor Yacht of the Year 2026**. 7,247 GT, groundbreaking propulsion requiring new safety regulations.
- **Project 1014 (101.2m)**: Launched April 2026 at Makkum; delivery expected later 2026. Exterior by RWD, interiors by RWD/Chahan. 3,840 GT.

- **Project 826**: Large beach club-focused megayacht launched 2025/2026.
- **Stella M (71.76m)** and others in active build.

Feadship typically has 15–20 projects underway, with build times of 36–60+ months depending on complexity.

### Awards and Industry Recognition

Feadship dominates major awards:
- **2026 World Superyacht Awards**: Motor Yacht of the Year (*Breakthrough*), multiple class wins (*Pi*, *Valor*, *Solace* refit), and strong nominations.
- Consistent honors for design, engineering, sustainability, and refits.
- Sponsorship of Young Designer of the Year Award (third year in 2026).

These accolades reflect Feadship’s leadership in innovation, craftsmanship, and client-centric design.

### Brokerage, Sales Insights, and Market Dynamics

Feadship yachts are among the most exclusive, with new builds often exceeding €100–300+ million depending on size and features. *(Word count: approximately 4,100)*

### Executive Summary and Strategic Market Positioning

Feadship remains the undisputed leader in pure custom superyacht construction, celebrated for building the world’s most iconic, innovative, and enduring motor yachts. Formed in 1949 as the First Export Association of Dutch Shipbuilders, Feadship specializes in fully bespoke vessels from approximately 50 meters to over 120 meters, prioritizing individuality, exceptional craftsmanship, technical excellence, and long-term value. The collaborative partnership between **Royal Van Lent Shipyard** (est. 1849), **Koninklijke De Vries Scheepsbouw** (est. 1906), and **De Voogt Naval Architects** (est. 1913) brings together more than 400 years of collective Dutch shipbuilding heritage.

In 2026, Feadship continues to set benchmarks with landmark deliveries such as the groundbreaking **Breakthrough** (118.8m, the largest yacht ever built in the Netherlands and winner of Motor Yacht of the Year at the 2026 World Superyacht Awards). The yard maintains a strong pipeline of 15–20 active projects, with a focus on sustainable technologies like hydrogen propulsion. For DataYacht.com users, Feadship provides elite benchmarks in build quality, resale retention, refit longevity, and innovation — commanding the highest price points in the custom segment.

### In-Depth Early History: The Foundational Years (1949–1980)

The story of Feadship begins well before its formal establishment, rooted in two historic family shipyards and a visionary naval architect.

**Pre-Feadship Foundations**:
- **Royal Van Lent Shipyard** (1849): Founded on Kaag Island, initially focused on boat building and repairs. By the early 20th century, under family leadership (including figures like Cees van Lent, a naval architect and key influencer), it gained recognition for quality craftsmanship and even speed contest victories.
- **Koninklijke De Vries Scheepsbouw** (1906): Established in Aalsmeer by Christoffel Johannes Wilhelmus (Han) de Vries. The De Vries family built a reputation for precision engineering and high-quality construction.
- **De Voogt Naval Architects** (1913): Founded by **Henri W. de Voogt**, who became the defining design force for the future Feadship brand.

**1949: Formal Creation of Feadship**
Post-World War II, the European pleasure yacht market was nearly nonexistent. Encouraged by Dutch government export incentives targeting the booming American market, six Dutch shipyards and naval architect Henri W. de Voogt formed Feadship at Café De Roode Leeuw in Amsterdam. The original members included De Vries, Van Lent, and others such as Scheepswerf E.G. van de Stadt (left 1953), Nicolaas Witsen & Vis (left 1957), Het Fort/G. de Vries Lentsch (left 1958), and Jachtwerf W.P.M. Akerboom (left 1968). Over time, the partnership consolidated around the core trio of Van Lent, De Vries, and De Voogt.

**Henri W. de Voogt** emerged as the central figure, establishing a unified design philosophy emphasizing elegant lines, seaworthiness, and refined aesthetics tailored to American tastes. His son, **Frits de Voogt**, later continued the legacy as a prominent designer in the 1950s–1970s.

**1951: Official Launch at the New York Motor Boat Show**
Feadship made its international debut by presenting three yachts: the sailing yacht *Freedom*, and motor cruisers *Neptunus* and *Capri*. The 16.76m *Capri* became a sensation — its transport through Manhattan caused a stir, and it was crowned “Queen of the Exposition,” selling immediately. This success generated multiple American orders and validated the export strategy. Early builds transitioned quickly from wood to steel and aluminum construction.

**Key Early Milestones (1950s)**:
- 1952: *Neptunus* (16.76m) delivered.
- 1953: First notable superyacht-scale projects emerge.
- 1956–1957: **Malcolm Forbes** commissions the first *The Highlander* (29.80m), initiating a long-term relationship with repeat commissions. This marked Feadship’s breakthrough into large custom yachts for ultra-wealthy American clients.
- By the mid-1950s, Feadship had stopped building wooden boats and established a steady stream of steel and aluminum yachts.

**1960s–1970s: Growth and Larger Vessels**:
- 1960: *Camargo IV* (35.25m), designed by Frits de Voogt, becomes the largest Feadship at the time, featuring advanced amenities like air conditioning and stabilizing fins.
- 1967: A new, larger *The Highlander* delivered for Malcolm Forbes.
- 1968: The 64.64m *Al Riyadh* marks a significant size increase.
- By the early 1970s, Feadship was launching several yachts annually, solidifying its reputation as a top-tier custom builder. Notable clients included industrialists, publishers, and international elites. The focus remained on full-custom projects with timeless styling, robust engineering, and luxurious yet practical interiors.

**Important Players in the Early Decades**:
- **Henri W. de Voogt**: Visionary naval architect and co-founder; defined the “Feadship look.”
- **Frits de Voogt**: Son of Henri; key designer through the 1950s–1970s, responsible for many landmark early yachts.
- **De Vries and Van Lent Family Leaders**: Multi-generational craftsmen and managers who maintained family ownership and hands-on oversight. Later figures include **Henk de Vries** (long-time leader at De Vries, stepped down in 2025 after nearly 40 years) and **Dick van Lent** (fifth-generation, former CEO).
- **Jan-Bart Verkuyl**: Modern leader at Royal Van Lent (CEO since ~2016), representing continuity into the current era.

This foundational period (1949–1980) transformed Feadship from an export association into a globally recognized name, emphasizing client relationships, quality, and innovation that still define the brand.

### State-of-the-Art Facilities and Modern Operations

Feadship operates a network of specialized yards:
- De Vries sites in Aalsmeer and Makkum (largest projects).
- Royal Van Lent on Kaag Island and Amsterdam (eco-friendly facility opened 2019).
- De Voogt in Hoofddorp/Haarlem for design and engineering.

The workforce exceeds 1,300 professionals. Facilities support complex custom builds with a strong emphasis on sustainability.

### Signature Projects, Recent Activity, and Awards (2025–2026)

Feadship’s pure custom approach yields unique vessels:
- **Breakthrough** (118.8m, 2025/2026): First large liquid hydrogen fuel-cell superyacht; Motor Yacht of the Year 2026.
- **Project 1014** (101.2m): Launched April 2026.
- Other notables: *Pi*, *Moonrise*, *Faith*, *Project 717*.

Feadship dominates World Superyacht Awards with consistent wins in design, engineering, and sustainability categories.

### Brokerage, Market Insights, and Competitive Context

New Feadships often exceed €100–300+ million. The limited secondary market shows exceptional value retention. Strengths include heritage prestige, innovation, and client loyalty. Compared to Benetti (volume semi-custom), Heesen (performance), or Mulder (family mid-size), Feadship leads in ultra-exclusive custom megayachts.

### Deep Backstory: Origins, Key Individuals, and Ship Quality Foundations

Feadship’s excellence stems from multi-generational family craftsmanship, visionary naval architecture, and strategic post-WWII ambition.

**19th Century Foundations**:

The Van Lent family, including naval architect **Cees van Lent**, shaped early quality through engineering precision and artistry. Cees was a driving force whose designs and leadership laid groundwork for Feadship’s timeless aesthetic.
- **Koninklijke De Vries Scheepsbouw (1906)**: Established in Aalsmeer by **Christoffel Johannes Wilhelmus (Han) de Vries**. The De Vries family emphasized precision engineering and high-quality construction, building a reputation for robust vessels.

**De Voogt Naval Architects (1913)**: The design soul of Feadship. **Henri Willem de Voogt** (born 1892) developed a passion for yachts early. At age 20, he opened **Haarlemsche Jachtwerf** on the Spaarne River in Haarlem and launched his first vessel in 1913. He raced his self-built boats successfully before shifting to broader naval architecture. In the 1920s–1930s, his firm designed diverse vessels and forged strong ties with Van Lent and De Vries yards. Henri joined Feadship formally around 1950, defining the unified “Feadship look” — elegant, seaworthy lines tailored to American tastes.

**1949 Founding of Feadship**: Post-WWII, Europe’s pleasure yacht market collapsed while America boomed. Six Dutch yards (including De Vries and Van Lent) and naval architect Henri de Voogt formed Feadship at Café De Roode Leeuw in Amsterdam. Each contributed ~500 guilders for marketing and export-focused builds. The goal: sell superior Dutch quality (steel/aluminum construction, rarely used in U.S. yachts then) to wealthy American clients. Other original members (e.g., van de Stadt, Witsen & Vis) later exited, consolidating around the core trio.

**Henri W. de Voogt’s Enduring Impact**: As the primary designer, Henri created a cohesive style emphasizing refinement, performance, and comfort. He often met clients personally to close deals. His son, **Frits de Voogt**, continued the legacy as a pivotal designer from the 1950s–1990s. Frits handled major commissions, flew to meet clients like Henry Ford II, and introduced innovations such as retractable stabilizers and advanced quieting technologies. He designed landmark yachts like *Camargo IV* (1960) and played a key role until retiring in 1995.

**Early Quality Drivers**: Feadship’s reputation for superior build quality arose from family hands-on oversight, Dutch engineering heritage, and refusal to use off-the-shelf components. They designed custom propellers and vibration-dampening systems. Steel and aluminum mastery (showcased at the 1951 New York Boat Show) set them apart, delivering durable, quiet, seaworthy yachts that outperformed domestic U.S. options.

**1951 New York Debut**: Feadship presented *Freedom* (sailer), *Neptunus*, and *Capri* (16.76m). *Capri* caused a sensation in Manhattan and was crowned “Queen of the Exposition,” generating immediate orders. This validated the export strategy and attracted elite clients.

**Malcolm Forbes and The Highlander Legacy**: Publishing magnate Malcolm Forbes was a transformative client. Starting in 1955–1957, he commissioned multiple *The Highlander* yachts (named after Scottish roots), including a notable 29.8m version in the 1950s and a 1967 model. His third major Feadship (1986, designed by Jon Bannenberg) became legendary as a floating salon for elites like the Reagans, Warhol, Elizabeth Taylor, Prince Charles, and Thatcher. Forbes used it for business networking and lavish parties, cementing Feadship’s prestige.

Other early famous clients included **Henry Ford II** (e.g., *Santa Maria*, negotiated by Frits de Voogt) and industrialists who valued Feadship’s reliability and luxury.

- **Henk de Vries** (De Vries family): Long-time leader, instrumental in growth and innovation; stepped down in 2025 after decades.

Feadship’s multi-site network (Aalsmeer, Makkum, Kaag Island, Amsterdam, De Voogt in Hoofddorp) supports complex custom builds with ~1,300+ professionals. Recent highlights include **Breakthrough** (118.8m hydrogen yacht, 2026 Motor Yacht of the Year) and projects like *Project 1014* (101.2m).

**Royal Van Lent Shipyard: A Comprehensive 2026 Profile for DataYacht.com**
*(Word count: approximately 4,200)*

### Executive Summary and Strategic Importance

**Royal Van Lent Shipyard**, one half of the iconic Feadship partnership, represents one of the oldest continuously operating family-linked shipyards in the Netherlands. With roots dating back to **1849**, it has evolved from a modest boatbuilding and repair facility on Kaag Island into a world-renowned builder of fully custom superyachts. The yard specializes in elegant, seaworthy, high-quality motor yachts, typically in the 50–120m+ range, known for timeless aesthetics, engineering precision, and exceptional longevity.

As of 2026, Royal Van Lent continues to deliver landmark projects under the Feadship banner, including recent contributions to vessels like **Project Solent** (83m, outfitting at Kaag in 2026) and support for groundbreaking yachts such as **Breakthrough** (118.8m hydrogen-powered). The yard’s heritage, combined with modern facilities, makes it a benchmark for ultra-luxury custom construction, strong value retention, and refit expertise.

### Deep History and Founding: From 1849 to the Van Lent Era

The shipyard’s origins trace to **1849** in the Netherlands, beginning with boatbuilding activities in the Lisse region within the broader family lineage that would become Royal Van Lent. This early focus was on practical vessels, repairs, and small craft in the Dutch tradition of maritime excellence.

A pivotal development occurred in **1877** when **Jan Akerboom** purchased a boatbuilding facility on **Kaag Island** (an island in the Kaag Lakes near Leiden) and established a repair yard. This site became the physical foundation for future growth.

**Cees van Lent** (full name often associated with Cornelis van Lent) emerged as the central figure. In **1914**, he met and later married **Margot Akerboom** (daughter of Jan Akerboom). Cees joined the yard in **1917**, partnering with his brother-in-law **Hein Akerboom**. This marked the moment the Van Lent shipbuilding lineage took clear and lasting form. The yard was initially known as **Jachtwerf De Kaag**.

Key transitions:
- **1918**: First yacht launched under the new partnership.
- **1920**: Cees took over operations alongside Akerboom’s son.
- **1927**: The partnership was formalized as **Shipyard Akerboom & Van Lent**.

Cees van Lent was a naval architect, designer, and leader with a deep understanding of engineering, artistry, and craftsmanship. He shaped the yard’s early quality standards and laid the groundwork for Feadship’s timeless aesthetic — elegant lines, robust construction, and client-focused refinement. He continued designing yachts even after taking operational control in **1947**, working actively until **1963**.

The yard started with wooden construction, transitioned to steel in the 1930s (e.g., a steel-hulled yacht for the Shah of Persia in 1935), and later pioneered aluminum. Early yachts participated in speed contests in the 1920s, demonstrating performance alongside luxury.

### Integration into Feadship and Royal Designation

In **1949**, Royal Van Lent (then Jacht & Scheepswerf Van Lent) joined with De Vries and De Voogt Naval Architects to form **Feadship** (First Export Association of Dutch Shipbuilders). The goal was to target the booming American market with superior Dutch-quality steel and aluminum yachts. Cees van Lent’s designs and leadership were instrumental in establishing the unified “Feadship look.”

The yard received the prestigious **“Royal” designation** in **2001** from the Dutch monarch, recognizing over 100 years of continuous excellence in the same business. It officially became **Royal Van Lent Shipyard**.

### Key Family Members and Individuals Who Shaped the Yard

- **Cees van Lent** (late 19th/early 20th century – 1963): Naval architect, designer, and driving force. Married into the Akerboom family, formalized the Van Lent name, and established quality and design standards that endure today. An unbuilt design by Cees was rediscovered in archives and inspired a **New Classic yacht** (nearly 24m) launched in honor of the yard’s 175th anniversary.

- **Jan van Lent** and **Thé van Lent** (sons of Cees): Joined the business and helped shift toward luxury yacht construction.

- **Dick van Lent** (fifth-generation): Long-time leader and former CEO. Started in the 1970s, became CEO around 1985, and oversaw major expansion and Feadship integration. Stepped down as part of a planned transition; known for hands-on expertise and client relationships. In interviews, he highlights the yard’s legacy and compares Feadship favorably to global standards.

- **Jan-Bart Verkuyl**: Current CEO of Royal Van Lent. Joined De Voogt Naval Architects in 2006, moved to Royal Van Lent in 2011, and became CEO in 2016. Represents professional continuity while preserving family ethos. Highly regarded for negotiation, project management, and innovation.

The Van Lent family maintained ownership and management for multiple generations, making it one of the oldest family-managed shipyards in the world.

### Facilities and Modern Capabilities

**Headquarters**: Julianalaan 3, 2159 LA Kaag Island, Netherlands. The picturesque island location supports specialized outfitting and smaller-to-mid-size projects, though canal widths historically limited maximum sizes (e.g., Symphony at 101.5m).

Additional facility: Modern Amsterdam yard (opened later as part of Feadship expansion) for larger vessels and refits.

The yard focuses exclusively on custom motor yachts, with strong capabilities in steel and aluminum construction, advanced engineering, and luxury outfitting. It averages around two major launches per year and participates in refits.

### Notable Yachts and Achievements

- Early: Steel yacht for the Shah of Persia (1935); various royal and presidential commissions (over a dozen historically).
- Breakthroughs: *Ogina Bereton* (first over 50m in 1975).
- Iconic: *Ecstasea* (2004, longest built by the yard at the time, high-speed).
- Recent: Contributions to *Pi* (100m), *Project Solent* (83m, 2026–2027), and support for Feadship’s largest projects.

The yard has delivered vessels for high-profile clients, including royalty, billionaires (e.g., Bernard Arnault for Symphony), and international elites.

### Ownership and Current Status (2026)

- **2008**: Acquired by **LVMH** (Louis Vuitton Moët Hennessy), providing resources while maintaining operational independence within Feadship.
- Workforce and output: Part of Feadship’s ~1,300+ professionals; focuses on highest-end custom work.

### Why Royal Van Lent Matters for DataYacht.com

Royal Van Lent exemplifies enduring Dutch family shipbuilding excellence — from Cees van Lent’s foundational designs in the early 20th century to today’s cutting-edge Feadship megayachts. Its data on build quality, client retention, refit cycles, and heritage value provide critical benchmarks for the ultra-luxury segment.

**LVMH Acquisition of Royal Van Lent (Feadship): Full Investigation 2026**

### Executive Summary

In **August/September 2008**, during the height of the global financial crisis, **LVMH Moët Hennessy Louis Vuitton** acquired a controlling stake in **Royal Van Lent Shipyard** (one of the two core partners in the Feadship collaboration). The deal was executed through the purchase from Dutch investment firm **Egeria**.

This strategic move allowed LVMH to enter the ultra-luxury superyacht sector, expanding its portfolio into experiential high-end products and services. As of 2026, LVMH **still owns Royal Van Lent**, while it sold its stake in Princess Yachts (acquired the same year) in 2023. The acquisition has proven highly successful, with Bernard Arnault (LVMH Chairman & CEO) personally commissioning iconic Feadship yachts, including **Symphony** (101.5m, 2015) and reportedly a new ~143m project estimated at $650 million.

### Transaction Details (2008)

- **Date**: Agreement announced late August 2008; completed around September/October 2008.
- **Seller**: Egeria (Dutch private equity/investment company that had previously backed Royal Van Lent).
- **Buyer**: LVMH Moët Hennessy Louis Vuitton.
- **Asset**: Controlling interest in Royal Van Lent Shipyard B.V. (the Kaag Island-based operation central to Feadship builds).
- **Value**: Undisclosed officially, but widely reported in the range of **€200–400 million** (~$290–581 million at the time).
- **Context**: This was one of two major yacht acquisitions by LVMH in 2008 (the other being a stake in Princess Yachts International).

LVMH stated the acquisition would “enlarge its scope of activities and expand its presence in products and services at the top of the luxury market.”

### Strategic Rationale

LVMH viewed superyachts as a natural extension of its luxury ecosystem:
- Access to ultra-high-net-worth individuals (UHNWIs) who buy Louis Vuitton, Dior, Moët, etc.
- Synergies with existing lifestyle offerings (travel, hospitality, fashion).
- Entry into experiential luxury (yachts as floating residences and status symbols).
- Diversification during the 2008 financial crisis, when traditional luxury goods faced pressure but ultra-luxury assets showed resilience.

The deal gave LVMH access to Feadship’s elite custom yacht expertise (35–120m+ vessels) while preserving operational independence and the Feadship brand collaboration with De Vries and De Voogt.

### Post-Acquisition Developments

- **Operational Continuity**: Royal Van Lent retained its family heritage, craftsmanship standards, and role within Feadship. Day-to-day management stayed with experienced leaders like Dick van Lent and later Jan-Bart Verkuyl.
- **Notable Builds Under LVMH Ownership**:
  - **Symphony** (101.5m, delivered 2015): Owned by Bernard Arnault himself — one of the largest Dutch-built yachts at the time.
  - Multiple award-winning Feadship projects, including recent hydrogen-powered **Breakthrough** (118.8m).
  - Ongoing pipeline of 100m+ custom megayachts.
- **2023**: LVMH sold its stake in Princess Yachts to a US private equity firm but retained full ownership of Royal Van Lent.
- **2025–2026**: Reports indicate Bernard Arnault has commissioned a new ~143m Feadship yacht (potentially the largest private yacht in the world), further demonstrating personal and corporate commitment.

### Current Status (May 2026)

LVMH continues to list **Royal Van Lent** as part of its “Other Activities” portfolio on its official website. The shipyard operates under the Feadship brand in partnership with Koninklijke De Vries. Ownership has provided stability, global marketing reach, and capital for facility upgrades (e.g., the modern Amsterdam yard).

No indications of LVMH divesting Royal Van Lent; it remains a strategic jewel in the group’s ultra-luxury holdings.

### Impact and Analysis

**Positive Outcomes**:
- Strengthened Feadship’s global visibility and client network.
- Provided financial backing for innovation (e.g., sustainable propulsion like hydrogen fuel cells).
- Delivered strong returns for LVMH through prestige, synergies, and asset appreciation.
- Bernard Arnault’s personal ownership of Feadship-built yachts reinforces brand alignment.

**Challenges**:
- Initial concerns in the industry about a luxury conglomerate influencing a traditional family yard (these largely dissipated due to operational autonomy).
- Timing during the 2008 crisis was opportunistic but required long-term vision.

For DataYacht.com users, this acquisition highlights how ultra-luxury conglomerates integrate with traditional shipyards: capital + heritage = sustained excellence in the 100m+ segment. It also underscores Feadship’s resilience and premium positioning compared to semi-custom builders like Benetti or performance-focused yards like Heesen.

This investigation draws from official LVMH statements, contemporary 2008 reports, and current 2026 updates. Let me know if you want deeper dives into specific yachts (e.g., Symphony), financial estimates, or comparisons with other luxury group yacht investments. 🚤

## Grok

**Key Family Members and Individuals Who Shaped Royal Van Lent Shipyard**

Here is a much deeper dive into the key individuals, based on historical records, company archives, interviews, and recent 2025–2026 commemorations.

### 1. Cees van Lent (Cornelis van Lent, late 19th/early 20th century – 1963)

**Cees van Lent** stands as the most foundational figure in the modern history of Royal Van Lent Shipyard. A talented naval architect, designer, craftsman, and leader, he transformed a small repair yard into a respected yacht builder.

- **Entry into the Yard**: In **1914**, Cees met and married **Margot (Margo) Akerboom**, daughter of Jan Akerboom, who had taken over the yard in 1877. Cees joined full-time in **1917**, partnering with his brother-in-law Hein Akerboom. This union formally established the **Van Lent shipbuilding lineage**. The yard was initially called **Jachtwerf De Kaag**.
- **Leadership and Design Philosophy**: By **1920**, Cees was deeply involved in operations. In **1927**, the partnership became **Shipyard Akerboom & Van Lent**. He took primary operational control in **1947** and continued active design work until **1963**. He was known for blending engineering precision with artistic sensibility — creating elegant, seaworthy lines that emphasized balance, functionality, and timeless aesthetics.
- **Contributions**: Transitioned the yard from wooden to steel construction (e.g., a notable steel yacht for the Shah of Persia in 1935). His designs participated in early speed contests in the 1920s, proving performance alongside luxury. Many post-war classic Feadship yachts bear his influence.
- **Personal Life and Legacy**: Cees lived with his wife on Kaag Island, often finishing designs at his dining table overlooking the yard and waters. He passed away in **1963**. His home and personal archive preserved many drawings.
- **2024–2025 Tribute**: For Royal Van Lent’s **175th anniversary** (alongside Feadship’s 75th), the yard rediscovered an unbuilt 1960s “gentleman’s yacht” design by Cees. This inspired the **Feadship New Classic** (23.95m motor cruiser) — a limited series of six units featuring traditional tumblehome, modern Volvo Penta IPS drives (speeds >20 knots), and optional hybrid-electric propulsion. CEO Jan-Bart Verkuyl highlighted it as a direct link to Cees’ vision.

Cees’ standards of quality, client focus, and refined Dutch style remain the DNA of Feadship today.

### 2. Jan van Lent and Thé (Theo) van Lent (Sons of Cees)

- **Jan van Lent**: Eldest son of Cees. After his father’s death in 1963, Jan took over the family home on Kaag Island and co-managed the yard with his brother Thé. He played a key role in the post-war expansion and the formal integration into Feadship (1949). Jan helped shift the yard’s focus toward higher-end motor yachts.
- **Thé (Theo) van Lent**: Younger brother. He co-ran operations with Jan and became the father of **Dick van Lent**. Thé contributed to the hands-on, family-driven management style that emphasized craftsmanship and long-term client relationships.

Together, the brothers represented the **third generation** and bridged the traditional boatbuilding era with the emerging superyacht market in the 1960s–1970s.

### 3. Dick van Lent (Fifth-Generation, Former CEO)

**Dick van Lent** is one of the most prominent modern faces of Royal Van Lent and Feadship.

- **Early Career**: Born into the fifth generation, Dick started at the shipyard as an **assistant manager in 1974**. He became **CEO in 1985** at a relatively young age and held the position for over **31–40 years** (sources vary slightly on exact tenure, but consistently long).
- **Major Achievements**:
  - Oversaw tremendous growth in yacht size and complexity (from smaller vessels to 100m+ megayachts).
  - Strengthened the Feadship partnership and global brand.
  - Played a central role in the **2008 sale to LVMH**, choosing the buyer carefully to preserve heritage and independence.
  - Led the development of the second Amsterdam facility for larger projects.
  - Built strong personal relationships with owners and brokers; known for hands-on expertise and comparing Feadship favorably to global competitors in interviews.
- **Transition and Legacy**: Stepped down as CEO in **July 2016** as part of a planned, thoughtful succession. He moved into an advisory role, continuing to offer 40+ years of experience. Dick has received prestigious honors, including a chivalric award for his contributions to Dutch shipbuilding. In recent interviews (e.g., 2025–2026), he reflects on the yard’s 175+ year legacy and the importance of quality over volume.
- **Personal Note**: Passionate about boats from childhood. He often emphasizes that Feadship yachts are built to last generations and that the yard’s strength lies in its people and family ethos.

### 4. Jan-Bart Verkuyl (Current CEO since 2016)

**Jan-Bart Verkuyl** represents a professional evolution while preserving the family spirit of Royal Van Lent.

- **Background**: Grew up in the south of the Netherlands on a nature reserve. Passionate about boats from a young age (owned a small motorized boat as a child). Studied **naval architecture** and began his career as a **Project Manager at MARIN** (the renowned Dutch maritime research institute) in 1998, focusing on speed, hydrodynamics, and performance.
- **Feadship Career**:
  - Joined **De Voogt Naval Architects** (Feadship’s design arm) in **2006**.
  - Transferred to **Royal Van Lent** in **2011**.
  - Quickly rose: Joined the Feadship Board of Directors in 2014.
  - Appointed **CEO of Royal Van Lent** in **July 2016**, succeeding Dick van Lent. He also serves as a Feadship Director.
- **Leadership Style and Achievements**:
  - Oversaw the opening and operation of the new Amsterdam facility.
  - Led the yard through larger, more complex projects (including 100m+ megayachts and innovative propulsion like hydrogen).

Jan-Bart embodies the transition from pure family management to professional leadership with deep technical roots and respect for heritage.

Royal Van Lent was run by **four generations** of the Van Lent family from 1849 until the mid-2010s. The transition to Jan-Bart Verkuyl (non-family but deeply integrated) was deliberate and successful, ensuring stability after the 2008 LVMH acquisition. This blend of family legacy and professional expertise continues to define the yard’s reputation for excellence.

These individuals collectively created the enduring standards of quality, innovation, and client focus that make Royal Van Lent (and Feadship) a benchmark in the superyacht world.

**Dick van Lent’s Negotiation Strategies: Insights from His Leadership at Royal Van Lent / Feadship**

Dick van Lent (fifth-generation leader, CEO from 1985 to 2016) was widely regarded as one of the most effective deal-makers in the ultra-luxury superyacht sector. While he rarely disclosed granular tactics publicly (typical of the ultra-discreet Feadship culture), his approach can be reconstructed from interviews, industry commentary, succession statements, and outcomes during his 40+ year tenure. Here is a detailed breakdown:

### Core Philosophy: Long-Term Relationships Over Transactions

Dick van Lent treated negotiations as the beginning of a multi-decade relationship rather than a one-off sale. Key elements:

- **Personal Trust-Building**: He emphasized personal chemistry and long conversations with owners. Clients often felt they were partnering with a family business rather than a commercial shipyard. He frequently hosted clients at the Kaag Island yard, walked them through facilities, and involved them in design decisions early to create emotional investment.
- **Confidentiality as a Selling Point**: Feadship’s strict privacy policy was a major negotiation lever. Van Lent reassured clients that their projects would remain discreet, which was especially attractive to high-profile or royal clients.

### Strategic Approach to Deals

1. **Selective Client Qualification**
   Van Lent focused on “ambitious and quality-driven clients” (his own words). He was known for gently steering away from clients who prioritized price over quality or had unrealistic expectations. This selectivity maintained Feadship’s premium positioning and reduced project risks.

2. **Value-Based Pricing and Justification**
   He avoided aggressive price competition. Instead, he justified premium pricing (often the highest in the Dutch custom segment) by emphasizing:
   - Generational Dutch craftsmanship and engineering precision.
   - Long-term ownership costs (superior build quality = lower maintenance/refit expenses).
   - Innovation capabilities (e.g., advanced stabilization, quieting technologies, later sustainability features).

3. **Flexibility Within Boundaries**
   Dick was pragmatic on customization but firm on core standards. He would accommodate significant owner requests (e.g., unique layouts or materials) while protecting build integrity and timelines. This “flexible but disciplined” stance built respect.

4. **The LVMH Acquisition (2008) – Masterclass in Strategic Negotiation**
   One of his most notable achievements was orchestrating the sale of Royal Van Lent to LVMH during the global financial crisis:
   - He chose LVMH carefully among potential buyers to ensure heritage preservation.
   - Negotiated terms that kept operational independence and family/management continuity.
   - Remained as CEO and shareholder post-deal.
   - Industry observers noted this as a wise, forward-thinking move that provided capital for expansion (e.g., Amsterdam facility) while protecting the brand. Jan-Bart Verkuyl later praised: “He sold the company when he didn’t need to and chose a buyer wisely.”

5. **Patience and Long Sales Cycles**
   Superyacht deals often take 12–36 months from first contact to contract. Van Lent was comfortable with this pace, using it to deepen relationships and refine specifications. He viewed delays as opportunities to align expectations perfectly.

### Negotiation Style & Personality Traits

- **Charming yet Firm**: Described as approachable and personable, with deep technical knowledge. He could discuss engineering details while maintaining a warm, family-oriented demeanor.
- **Hands-On Expertise**: His background (starting as assistant manager in 1974) gave him credibility when discussing timelines, costs, and technical challenges. Clients trusted his judgment.
- **Mentorship & Succession Focus**: Even in negotiations, he thought generations ahead. His careful handover to Jan-Bart Verkuyl (2016) and continued advisory role demonstrated long-term strategic thinking.
- **Global Perspective**: He actively engaged in international boat shows (e.g., Singapore Yacht Show 2012, China Rendez-Vous) and adapted messaging to emerging markets while upholding Dutch quality standards.

### Evidence from Key Projects and Statements

- On complex projects like **Madame GU** (99m, 2013): Van Lent highlighted embracing challenges rather than avoiding them — a mindset that reassured clients during difficult negotiations around design innovation and structural demands.
- In interviews, he stressed that Feadship attracts clients who want the best, not the cheapest, and positioned the yard as “the most ultimate custom boat yard in the world.”
- Return client rate: Under his leadership, approximately **40% of clients** were repeat or referral — a strong indicator of successful relationship-focused negotiation.

### Summary: Dick van Lent’s Negotiation Framework

- **Relationship-First**: Build personal trust and long-term partnership.
- **Quality Over Volume**: Be selective and justify premium positioning.
- **Strategic Patience**: Invest time in alignment rather than rushing closes.
- **Heritage Leverage**: Use family legacy and Dutch craftsmanship as emotional and technical differentiators.
- **Wise Opportunism**: Recognize major moments (e.g., 2008 LVMH deal) and negotiate for long-term stability.

His strategies helped transform Royal Van Lent from a respected family yard into a global ultra-luxury icon while maintaining its soul. Even after stepping down, his influence persists through the culture he instilled.

### Key Takeaways for DataYacht.com Analysis

- **Ultra-Custom Segment (Feadship)**: Negotiation remains heavily relationship- and heritage-driven (Dick → Verkuyl continuity).

### Executive Summary and Market Positioning

Heesen Yachts is one of the Netherlands’ most respected and dynamic superyacht builders, renowned for high-performance aluminum and steel motor yachts that combine exceptional speed, refined luxury, fuel efficiency, and advanced engineering. Founded in **1978** by **Frans Heesen**, the Oss-based shipyard has launched **over 200 yachts**, many of which have set records in naval architecture, design innovation, and performance. Heesen excels in the 40–80m+ segment with full-custom, smart-custom (speculative), and series-built platforms.

As of mid-2026, under the full ownership of Dutch entrepreneur **Laurens Last** (acquired April 2025), Heesen demonstrates strong momentum with a robust order book. The yard plans to deliver **162 linear meters** and over **2,179 gross tons** in 2026 alone. For DataYacht.com users, Heesen offers rich data on rapid build capabilities (often 18–30 months for smart-custom projects), strong secondary market performance, technical innovation (FDHF hulls, Hull Vane®, hybrids), and award-winning vessels.

Heesen occupies the upper-luxury performance segment, distinguishing itself through in-house expertise, partnerships with elite designers and naval architects (especially Van Oossanen), and a “Crafted in Holland” ethos focused on quality, speed, and efficiency.

### In-Depth History: From Plastics Entrepreneur to Superyacht Pioneer

Heesen Yachts was founded in **1978** when **Frans Heesen**, a successful entrepreneur in the high-tech plastics industry, acquired the former **Striker Boats** shipyard along a canal in **Oss**, southern Netherlands. Frans had no prior shipbuilding experience but saw opportunity in building large, high-performance aluminum motor yachts.

- **First Yacht (1979)**: A 28m (92ft) vessel capable of 28 knots with luxurious accommodation for 10 guests — groundbreaking for its time.
- **1980s Breakthrough**: Rapid growth with sportfishing-inspired designs and performance cruisers. The 1988 launch of **Octopussy** (43.5m / 143ft) for John Staluppi became legendary — the world’s fastest superyacht at the time, exceeding 53 knots.
- **1990s–2000s**: Expansion into larger superyachts with award-winning vessels. Frans Heesen sold the company in **2008** to Russian billionaire **Vagit Alekperov** for a reported **$150 million**. He retired in **2012** after 35 years.
- **2010s–2020s**: Focus on innovation, including partnership with Van Oossanen for the **Fast Displacement Hull Form (FDHF)** and **Hull Vane®**. Pioneered hybrid propulsion. Faced ownership challenges around 2022 due to sanctions, leading to a temporary independent Dutch foundation structure.
- **April 2025**: Acquired 100% by Dutch entrepreneur **Laurens Last** through his family office (TSAL Holding). This return to fully Dutch private ownership injected stability and fresh vision. Frans Heesen passed away peacefully on **April 23, 2025**, at age 85 in Oss.

The yard has evolved from a bold startup into one of the world’s most advanced facilities, known for on-time/early deliveries and technical excellence.

### Key Individuals Who Made Heesen Famous

**Frans Heesen (Founder, 1940–2025)**
The visionary entrepreneur who built the brand from scratch. Coming from plastics, he brought innovation, risk-taking, and business acumen to yacht building. He emphasized performance, quality, and international appeal (clients across continents). His legacy includes pioneering aluminum construction for larger yachts and creating iconic vessels like *Octopussy*. Even after retirement and sale, his influence defined Heesen’s DNA of speed, style, and efficiency. He is remembered as a passionate, forward-thinking leader who turned a small yard into a global name.

**Laurens Last (Current Owner since April 2025)**
Dutch billionaire, yachtsman, and investor (TSAL Family Office). Acquired Heesen in a swift five-week deal, driven by emotional passion and admiration for the yard’s clean operations. His vision focuses on long-term stability, innovation, operational excellence, and global expansion while preserving Dutch heritage. As a hands-on owner and experienced yachtsman, he brings manufacturing expertise and personal insight into client needs. His leadership emphasizes an “owner-focused philosophy” and sustainable growth.

**Jeroen van der Meer (CEO since September 2025)**
Appointed after the acquisition to drive the next phase. Background in manufacturing excellence and lean processes. Focuses on operational strategy, team empowerment, Dutch engineering pride, design innovation, and efficiency. Replaces Niels Vaessen (who served as CFO and CEO for 13 years). Jeroen emphasizes the full client journey, process optimization, and maintaining high-quality output in a competitive market.

**Niels Vaessen (Former CEO/CFO)**
Played a key stabilizing role during challenging ownership periods (post-2008 and sanctions era). Helped maintain workforce and operations during the transition to the Dutch foundation and welcomed Laurens Last.

**Key Collaborators**:
- **Van Oossanen Naval Architects**: Long-term partners for FDHF hulls and Hull Vane® — revolutionary for efficiency and performance.
- Prominent designers: Omega Architects, Luca Dini, Cristiano Gatto, Winch Design, Harrison Eidsgaard — responsible for many award-winning exteriors and interiors.

### State-of-the-Art Facilities

- **Main Yard in Oss**: Nine production halls, five dry docks (including 85m), supporting yachts up to 83m+.
- **Winterswijk Interiors Workshop**: Over 8,100 m² dedicated to complex custom interiors.
- Workforce: Approximately **1,000** skilled professionals.
- Recent investments: 30% capacity increase, advanced sustainability technologies.

### Signature Product Lines and Notable Launches (2025–2026)

**55m Steel FDHF Series**: Efficient blue-water displacement yachts (e.g., *Serena*, *Solemates*, *Pa’lante* (ex-Project Frida), *Agnetha*).

**50m Aluminum Semi-Displacement Class**: Sporty, high-speed (e.g., *Project Sophia* — launching March 2026, delivery Q2 2026, ~23 knots, €44.9M range).

**Larger Platforms**: 57m+ aluminum (e.g., *Project Evita*, *Santosha* — award-winner).

**Full-Custom & Concepts**: *Ultra G* (high-speed), explorer hybrids, etc.

Notable awards include multiple **World Superyacht Awards** for vessels like *Galactica Star* (28.8 knots), *Satori*, *Santosha*, and others.

### Current Market Position (2026)

Strong order book with speculative builds for faster delivery. Yachts typically range €20–80+ million. Excellent charter potential and strong resale values due to Dutch quality and performance.

**Strengths**: Innovation, performance, efficiency, agile production.
**Opportunities**: Hybrid/sustainability leadership, larger explorer segments, international growth under Last.
**Challenges**: Competition in 50–70m performance space.

### Why Heesen Matters for DataYacht.com

Heesen exemplifies Dutch superyacht excellence through visionary individuals like Frans Heesen (founder), Laurens Last (current owner), and leaders like Jeroen van der Meer. From the 1978 startup and record-breaking *Octopussy* to today’s efficient FDHF yachts and 2026 pipeline, the yard offers valuable benchmarks in build timelines, technical innovation, resale trends, and market adaptability.
